MELVYN B. STEIN

Grand Master
October 11, 2006 – September 30, 2007

ii

MELVYN B. STEIN

Grand Master – 2006-2007
Our Grand Master, MELVYN B. STEIN was born in Los Angeles,
California on September 12, 1941 and has resided in San Diego for over 60
years. In 1973 he married his lovely wife, Betty. They have two sons and a
daughter, all of whom have contributed to their five grandchildren.
Brother Mel has been a Master Mason for 43 years having been raised
in San Diego Lodge No. 35 where he served as Master in 1972. He is also a
member of Joseph L. Shell Daylight Lodge No. 837 and Lux Lodge No. 846.
Brother Stein has served the Grand Lodge in numerous positions
including:


1998/1999 as Grand Orator for Most Worshipful Melville H. Nahin


Trustee of the California Masonic Foundation; the Masonic Homes of
California; the California Masonic Memorial Temple and Nob Hill Corporation

Prior to his election as Junior Grand Warden he served on Grand Lodge
Committees for 28 years, including the following:
*
*
*
*
*
*

Policy & General Purposes
Jurisprudence
Trial Review
Research & Planning
Bylaws, as its Chairman
Masonic Management Workshop

In 1993 Brother Stein served as Venerable Master of the Lodge of
Perfection of the Scottish Rite Valley of San Diego and in 1995 as Assistant
Personal Representative of the Sovereign Grand Inspector General. He was
coroneted an Inspector General Honorary of the Thirty-Third Degree in 1991.
He is a life member of Royal Arch Masons, Cryptic Masons, Order of the
Eastern Star, National Sojourners and Hero’s of ‘76. He also holds the
DeMolay Legion of Honor and was “League Dad” for San Diego County.
In 2000 he became Potentate of Al Bahr Shrine (in San Diego) and is
still on the active Rosters of the Tin Lizzies and other shrine clubs.
In community affairs, Brother Stein served on the Board of Directors of
the San Diego/Imperial Council of the Girl Scouts; Chairman of the San Diego
Kiwanis Foundation; Board of Directors of the San Diego Kiwanis Club;
Director of “Laurels for Leaders”; President of the Harbor Optimist Club;

iii

Chairman of the La Mesa Unified School District Racial and Ethnic Balance
Committee; Board of Directors of Tifereth Israel Synagogue; and Director of
Pop Warner Football and Little League Baseball.
Brother Stein has a Bachelor of Business Administration Degree and a
Juris Doctor Degree. He continues his profession as an attorney in private
practice after 40 years now specializing in Family law, Wills and Probate. He
serves as a Judge Pro-Tem for the Superior Court and has been listed in “Who’s
Who in American Law”. He was also a member of both the California Superior
Court and Family Law Legislation Committees of the State Bar Association. He
is admitted to practice before all State and Federal Courts including the United
States Supreme Court.
Brethren, ladies, and friends, it is my pleasure to PRESENT the 142nd
Grand Master of Masons in California, the Most Worshipful Melvyn B. Stein.

and terminating on Sunday the Thirtieth of September, A.L. 6007.

MW Grand Lodge of Free and Accepted Masons of the State of California,

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

3

ONE HUNDRED FIFTY-EIGHTH
ANNUAL COMMUNICATION
FRIDAY AFTERNOON, SEPTEMBER 28, 2007
The 158th Annual Communication of the Grand Lodge of Free and
Accepted Masons of the State of California was called to order by Grand Master
Melvyn B. Stein, on Friday, September 28, 2007.
Worshipful Patrick W. Wohlrabe, Senior Warden of San Diego Lodge
No. 35, raised the opening session of Grand Lodge.

Reception of the Past Grand Masters
The Grand Master welcomed the brethren and directed the Grand
Marshal to present the Past Grand Masters of the Grand Lodge of California at
the Altar. The Grand Marshal then introduced each Past Grand Master,
following which the Grand Master, accompanied by the Deputy Grand Master,
personally greeted each one. The Grand Master asked the brethren to join him
in saluting our Past Grand Masters by the battery of three times three. The Past
Grand Masters were then escorted to seats reserved for them in the auditorium.
The Grand Master then directed the Grand Secretary to send to each absent Past
Grand Master the usual letter of greeting from the Grand Lodge.

Roll Call
The Grand Secretary was asked to call the roll of Grand Lodge
Officers, Past Grand Masters, and Past Grand Officers, and to ascertain if a
constitutional number was present to convene the Grand Lodge of California.
The following were present as indicated by their response to the calling of the
roll:

The Grand Secretary announced that the Committee on Credentials had
reported that there were 859 Master Masons registered as of 1:00 p.m. Friday,
with at least twenty percent of the Lodges chartered by this Grand Lodge
represented, and that therefore a quorum was present as required for the
transaction of business.
The Grand Secretary reported that all Lodges have paid their annual per
capita assessment to Grand Lodge except for the following Lodges:

Grand Lodge Opening

The Grand Master then opened Grand Lodge in ample form. The
Grand Marshal and the Deacons retired to escort the Flag of the United States of
America into the Auditorium, which was followed by the Pledge of Allegiance.

Introductions
The following were asked to rise as groups for recognition: Hiram
Award Recipients, Diamond Jubilee Award Recipients, Golden Veteran Award
Recipients, Worshipful Masters, Lodge Secretaries, Grand Lodge Committee
members, members of Constitutional Boards, District Inspectors, and Visiting
Brethren from other jurisdictions. Each group was greeted with a round of
applause.

7

The Grand Master welcomed the representatives to the Grand Lodge of
California, and asked them to convey to their respective Grand Masters best
wishes for the prosperity of Freemasonry in their Grand Lodges, and the
fraternal greetings of the Grand Lodge of California. He then asked the Grand
Marshal to afford the guests seats in the auditorium.

Grand Master’s Message
The Grand Master then relinquished his chair to the Deputy Grand
Master for the purpose of delivering the Grand Master’s Message (Appendix A).
After his message, the Grand Master received a standing ovation, following
which he resumed his station.

Grand Treasurer's Report
The Grand Master called on the Grand Treasurer, Very Worshipful
Glenn D. Woody, for his report (Appendix D).
Report of the Grand Secrtary
The Grand Master called on the Grand Secretary, John L. Cooper III,
for his report (Appendix E).
Report of the Grand Lecturer
The Grand Master called on the Grand Lecturer, Paul D. Hennig, for
his report (Appendix F).

Report of the Masonic Homes Board of Trustees
The Grand Master called on the President of the Masonic Homes Board
of Trustees, Most Worshipful Allen B. Gresham, PGM, for his report (Appendix
J).
Report of the California Masonic Foundation Board of Trustees
The Grand Master called on the President of the California Masonic
Foundation Board of Trustees, Worshipful John F. Lowe, for his report
(Appendix J).

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

Report of the California Masonic Memorial Temple Board of Trustees
The Grand Master called on the President of the California Masonic
Memorial Temple Board of Trustees, Worshipful David D. Johnson, for his
report (Appendix J).

Report of the Nob Hill Masonic Center Board of Trustees
The Grand Master called on the President of the California Masonic
Memorial Temple Board of Trustees, Most Worshipful M. William Holsinger,
Past Grand Master, for his report (Appendix J).

Report of the Committee on Trial Review
Worshipful Richard Meyers, Chairman, presented the written report of
the Trial Review Committee which can be found in its entirety in Appendix K.

Grand Master’s Decisions and Recommendation
Grand Master Stein’s Decision and Recommendation are to be found in
Appendix I, together with the Grand Master’s remarks on each one, and
committee reports where presented. The vote on each is also reported there.
The following summary of Grand Master Stein’s Decision and Recommendation
is provided, together with the final vote on each one:
The Grand Master called on the Chairman of the Jurisprudence
Committee, Most Worshipful R. Stephen Doan, Past Grand Master, for his
report (Appendix K).
The Grand Master then called on the Chairman of the Finance
Committee, Russell E. Charvonia, for his report (Appendix K).
Grand Master Stein then reminded the members of the established rules
and relinquished his chair to the Deputy Grand Master before presenting his
Decision and Recommendation.

Grand Oration

Ralph B. Dash, who presented the Grand Oration (Appendix H).

The Grand Master reiterated the rules governing the proper protocol
and manner of discussion on the various pieces of legislation.

Video Presentation
This year the Grand Master requested the Masonic Formation Task
Force to have prepared a DVD that can be given to a new Entered Apprentice
Mason to share with his wife, family and friends. Those who view the video
should gain a greater understanding of the significance of Freemasonry and its
impact upon a man, his family, and his community. The version shown is an
excerpt from the first draft of the DVD. A final version should be released at a
later date, at which time it will be made available to the Lodges.

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

SATURDAY AFTERNOON, SEPTEMBER 29, 2007
At 1:00 p.m. the Grand Master called the Grand Lodge from
refreshment to labor, following which the Grand Chaplain offered prayer. The
Grand Master then announced that the Grand Lodge had been raised for the
afternoon session by Brother Hans “Tracy” Delfo, a member of San Diego
Lodge No. 35.

Credentials Committee Report
The Grand Master called on the Grand Secretary for the final report of
the Credentials Committee. The Grand Secretary reported that there were 298
Lodges registered as being in attendance, and a total of 1,189 Master Masons
and others in attendance.

Continuation of Legislation
The Grand Master reiterated the rules governing the proper protocol
and manner of discussion on the various pieces of legislation.

Adoption of the 2007-2008 Grand Lodge Budget
The Grand Master called on the Deputy Grand Master, Right
Worshipful Richard W. Hopper, to present the 2007-2008 Grand Lodge Budget.
He did so, and a copy of the budget is found in Appendix B. The Chairman of
the Finance Committee, Worshipful Russ Charvonia, moved the adoption of the
2006-2007 Budget, which was seconded by Glenn D. Woody, Grand Treasurer,
and was carried by a majority affirmative vote.

Adoption of the 2008-2009 Grand Lodge Per Capita
The Grand Master then called on the Senior Grand Warden, Right
Worshipful Larry L. Adamson, to present the 2008-2009 Grand Lodge Per
Capita. He did so, and a copy of the proposal is found in Appendix C.
The Chairman of the Finance Committee, Worshipful Russ Charvonia,
moved the adoption of the 2008-2009 Grand Lodge Per Capita in the amount of
$31.00, which was seconded by Glenn D. Woody, Grand Treasurer, and carried
by a majority affirmative vote.

Election of Officers
The Grand Master then asked the Grand Secretary to explain the
balloting procedure for the election of officers. No ballot would be necessary if
the office were not contested. If more than one nomination were received for an
office, ballot cards would be distributed for the purpose of balloting for that
office.

Election of the Grand Master
Deputy Grand Master Richard W. Hopper was nominated Grand
Master by Most Worshipful Allen B. Gresham, Past Grand Master. No further
nominations appearing, it was moved, seconded and carried that the nominations
be closed, and that the Grand Secretary be instructed to cast a unanimous ballot

14

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

for Right Worshipful Richard W. Hopper to serve as Grand Master for the
ensuing Grand Lodge Year. Upon his acceptance, he was declared unanimously
elected.

Election of Deputy Grand Master
Senior Grand Warden Larry L. Adamson was nominated Deputy Grand
Master by Most Worshipful Charles H. Alexander, Past Grand Master. No
further nominations appearing, it was moved, seconded and carried that the
nominations be closed, and that the Grand Secretary be instructed to cast a
unanimous ballot for Right Worshipful Larry L. Adamson to serve as Deputy
Grand Master for the ensuing Grand Lodge Year. Upon his acceptance, he was
declared unanimously elected.

Election of Senior Grand Warden
Junior Grand Warden Kenneth G. Nagel was nominated Senior Grand
Warden by Most Worshipful Frederick L. Sorsabal, Past Grand Master. No
further nominations appearing, it was moved, seconded and carried that the
nominations be closed, and that the Grand Secretary be instructed to cast a
unanimous ballot for Right Worshipful Kenneth G. Nagel to serve as Senior
Grand Warden for the ensuing Grand Lodge Year. Upon his acceptance, he was
declared unanimously elected.

Election of Junior Grand Warden
The Grand Master announced that pursuant to Section 404.420 of the
California Masonic Code, Worshipful William J. Bray III, a Past Master of
North Hollywood Lodge No. 542 in Los Angeles had been recommended for the
office of Junior Grand Warden of the Grand Lodge of California for the ensuing
Grand Lodge Year. The Grand Secretary then read the biography previously
circulated in accordance with this section, while Brother Bray stood before
Grand Lodge. He was then escorted to the Grand Tiler’s Room, while other
nominations were called for. None appearing, the Junior Grand Warden moved
that the Grand Secretary be instructed to cast a unanimous ballot for Worshipful
Kenneth G. Nagel to serve as Junior Grand Warden for the ensuing Grand
Lodge Year. The motion was seconded by the Grand Lecturer and unanimously
carried. Upon his return to the floor of the Grand Lodge, and upon his
acceptance, he was declared unanimously elected by the Grand Master.

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

15

Election of Grand Treasurer, Grand Secretary, and Grand Lecturer
The Grand Master then announced that pursuant to Section 404.430 of
the California Masonic Code, the Executive Committee had recommended that
the following be nominated to serve for the ensuing Grand Lodge Year:
Grand Treasurer, Glenn D. Woody
Grand Secretary, John L. Cooper III
Grand Lecturer, Paul D. Hennig

Election of Grand Treasurer
Most Worshipful M. William Holsinger nominated Very Worshipful
Glenn D. Woody to serve as Grand Treasurer for the ensuring Grand Lodge
Year. No further nominations appearing, he moved that the nominations be
closed, and that the Grand Secretary be instructed to cast a unanimous ballot for
Glenn D. Woody to serve as Grand Treasurer. The motion was duly seconded
and adopted unanimously, and upon his acceptance, the Grand Master declared
him elected.

Election of Grand Secretary
Most Worshipful Stanley M. Cazneaux, Past Grand Master, nominated
Very Worshipful John L. Cooper III to serve as Grand Secretary for the ensuing
Grand Lodge Year. No further nominations appearing, he moved that the
nominations be closed, and that the Grand Treasurer be instructed to cast a
unanimous ballot for John L. Cooper III to serve as Grand Secretary. The
motion was duly seconded and adopted unanimously, and upon his acceptance,
the Grand Master declared him elected.

Election of Grand Lecturer
Very Worshipful Harold A. Macy, Past Grand Lecturer, nominated
Very Worshipful Paul D. Hennig to serve as Grand Lecturer for the ensuing
Grand Lodge Year. No futher nominations appearing, he moved that the
nominations be closed, and that the Grand Secretary be instructed to cast a
unanimous ballot for Paul D. Hennig to serve as Grand Lecturer. The motion
was duly seconded and adopted unanimously, and upon his acceptance, the
Grand Master declared him elected.

16

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

Grand Lodge Called from Labor to Refreshment
The Grand Master then called Grand Lodge from labor to refreshment
for the purpose of installing the Grand Lodge Officers for the ensuing Grand
Lodge Year. The Installation was announced as being at 11:00 a.m. on Sunday,
September 30, 2007.

20

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

Presentation of the Membership Development Award
In the period of July 1, 2006 to June 30, 2007, our Lodges conferred
4,346 degrees, which is more degrees than have been conferred in our
jurisdiction since 1989. However, our jurisdiction had 540 Lodges in 1989 that
conferred those degrees. Today we have 344. Our Lodges conferred this year
more degrees per Lodge than any year since 1983. In fact, we have conferred
more degrees than the previous year every year for the last seven years.
Our major gains have taken place over the last four years. In 2002, our
Lodges initiated 1,005 Entered Apprentice Masons. In 2007, we initiated 1,922
– a 92% percent increase! In 2003, we introduced the Pass It On program full of
new resources for Lodges and members to use in sharing Freemasonry with their
family, friends, and colleagues. As part of that program, a goal was set for each
Lodge to raise at least five new Master Masons a year. At that time, only 60
Lodges in the state had reached that milestone. This year over 100 Lodges have
accomplished that feat. Each of those Lodges will receive special recognition
from the Grand Lodge for their great work.
The Grand Master made a special presentation to Huntington Beach
Lodge No. 380 who during the last 12 months rasied a total of 27 Master
Masons.

Presentation of the Leadership Training Awards
The Lodge Management Certification Program consists of five courses
that are conducted simultaneously at a particular location or online. The courses
include:
Hall Association Management, Lodge Finance, Membership
Development, Program Planning, and Lodge Administration. Each course is a
full day in length. At the end of each course, participants must pass an
examination before they are given a certification in that course.
The Grand Master presented plaques to the following brothers who
have completed all five courses of the Lodge Management Certification
Program:
Brother Michael Glaser
Worshipful Brother Nicholas Mitchell
Worshipful Brother Thomas Spencer
Worshipful Brother Marvin Ens
Worshipful Brother Roy Steinfeld
Worshipful Brother Donald Young

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

21

Ritual Awards Presentation
“Ritual is an integral part of the foundation of Freemasonry. The great
lessons of the fraternity are found in and between the spoken words that we
deliver during our meetings and ceremonies. To promote high standards in
ritual presentation and performance, the Ritual Committee of the Grand Lodge
sponsors an annual Ritual competition. At his time, I call on our Grand
Lecturer, the Very Worshipful Paul Hennig, for the presentation of the Ritual
Competition awards.”

Presentation of the Daniel Carter Beard Masonic Scouter Award
“Community involvement is another top priority for Freemasonry in
California. Through our individual involvement in our communities, we
demonstrate to our fellow citizens the high purposes of Freemasonry. There are
many ways to be involved in the community including working with the
children in your neighborhood and through the several children's organizations
in your town.
The Grand Lodge of California has joined other Grand Lodges in this
country, in recognizing Master Masons who have provided outstanding service
to youth through Scouting with the Daniel Carter Beard Masonic Scouter
Award.
Daniel Beard founded a male youth program in the late 1800's called
the "Society of the Sons of Daniel Boone." Lord Robert Baden-Powell read of
Beard's program and developed what is now known as the "Boy Scouts."
The new Boy Scout program came to America in 1910, when Brother
Dan merged his organization into the "Boy Scouts of America" and became its
first National Commissioner.
Brother Dan was raised a Master Mason in Mariners Lodge No. 67,
New York City, New York.
Tonight, it is my pleasure to invest this award upon one of our
distinguished brothers. Please welcome to the podium, Brother Henry Wilson
Coil, Jr.”
The Grand Master then invited all to attend the Public Ceremonies and
the Installation of Officers on Sunday morning, September 30, 2007 beginning
at 9:30 a.m.

Memorial Service
The Grand Master called on the Very Reverend Maxwell S. Roth,
Grand Chaplain, to present a service of remembrance for our departed brethren.
“A butterfly lights beside us like a sunbeam
And for a brief moment its glory
And beauty belong to our world
But then it flies again
And though we wish it could have stayed
We feel lucky to have seen it.”
Author Unknown
The subject of death has been pondered, discussed and written about
through the centuries by all cultures and religions. I would like to share with
you some reading which come from different sources, but all of which deal with
the human wrestling we each must do with the fact that, for us and for those
whom we love, life has both a beginning and an end.
Centuries ago the Roman philosopher Seneca wrote: “In the presence
of death, we must continue to sing the song of life. We must be able to accept
death and go from its presence better able to bear our burdens and to lighten the
load of others. Out of our sorrows should come understanding. Through our
sorrows, we join with all of those before who have had to suffer and all of those
who will yet have to do so. Let us not be gripped by the fear of death. If
another day be added to our lives, let us joyfully receive it, but let us not
anxiously depend on our tomorrows. Though we grieve the deaths of our loved
ones, we accept them and hold on to our memories as precious gifts. Let us
make the best of our loved ones while they are with us, and let us not bury our
love with death.”
Joyce Cary wrote: “I look upon life as a gift from God; I did nothing to
earn it. Now that the time is coming to give it back, I have no right to
complain.”
Ibn Gabirol wrote: “Plan for this world as if you expect to live forever,
but plan for the hereafter as if you expect to die tomorrow.”
This is a prayer of sorts, from the American Indian tradition. It is
spoken by the one who has died. It is entitled “Life Must Go On” a Navaho
Prayer.
Grieve for me, for I would grieve for you.
Then brush away the sorrow and the tears
Life is not over, but begins anew,

24

PROCEEDINGS OF THE
With courage you must greet the coming years.
To live forever in the past is wrong;
Can only cause you misery and pain.
Dwell not on memories overlong,
With others you must share and care again.
Reach out and comfort those who comfort you;
Recall the years, but only for awhile.
Nurse not your loneliness; but live again.
Forget not. Remember with a smile.
Ashley Montaqu wrote: “The idea is to die young as late as possible.”

Dave Farber wrote: “We all have to die some day, if we live long
enough.”
James Whitcomb Riley wrote a short poem entitled “A Parting Guest”,
in which he describes a human being departing this life as if he were leaving a
party with thanks and gratitude to his/her hosts:
What delightful hosts are they
Life and Love!
Lingeringly I turn away,
This late hour, yet glad enough
They have not withheld from me
Their high hospitality.
So, with face lit with delight
And all gratitude, I stay
Yet to press their hands and say,
“Thanks. So fine a time! Good night.”
Two pieces from unknown writers:
Remember Me:
To the living, I am gone.
To the sorrowful, I will never return.
To the angry, I was cheated,
But to the happy, I am at peace,
And to the faithful, I have never left.
I cannot be seen, but I can be heard.
So as you stand upon a shore, gazing at a beautiful sea –
remember me.
As you look in awe at a might forest and its grand majesty –
remember me.

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

25

As you look upon a flower and admire its simplicity –
remember me.
Remember me in your heart, your thoughts, and your
memories of the times we loved, the times we cried, the times
we fought, the times we laughed.
For if you always think of me, I will have never gone.
When you wer born, you wer crying and everyone around you was
smiling. Live your life so that when you die, you’re smiling and everyone
around you is crying.
I quote from Gates of Repentance, “The New Union Prayerbook for the
Days of Awe.”
Birth is a beginning
And death a destination.
And life is a journey.
From childhood to maturity
And youth to age;
From innocence to awareness
And ignorance to knowing;
From foolishness to discretion
And then, perhaps, to wisdom;
From weakness to strength
Or strength to weakness
And, often, back again;
From health to sickness
And bacy, we pray, to health again;
From offense to forgiveness,
From loneliness to love,
From joy to gratitude, from pain to compassion,
And grief to understanding
From fear to faith;
From defeat to defeat to defeat
Until, looking backward or ahead,
We see that victory lies
Not at some high place along the way;
But in having made the journey, stage by stage,
A sacred pilgrimage.
Birth is a beginning.
And death a destination.
And life is a journey,
A sacred pilgrimage
To life everlasting.

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

27

EULOGY
ROBERT A. K LINGER
Robert Alan Klinger served the Masonic Grand Lodge of California as
Grand Secretary from October, 1980, until his retirement in October, 1991. He
was the ninth Grand Secretary since the founding of our Grand Lodge in 1850.
He entered into rest on June 18, 2007.
Bob was born on November 5, 1923, in San Francisco, California. He
was the son of Seth Alan Klinger and Genevieve Newman. His mother was a
girl of seven when the great San Francisco Earthquake and Fire of 1906
occurred, and Bob liked to tell the story his mother told him of her experiences
“camping out” in Golden Gate Park in the days that followed.
The history of San Francisco was Bob’s history as well, and he was
proud of his native city. His mother’s father, Sam Newman, was a founder and
partner in Redlich- Newman, a large furniture store in San Francisco, which
opened in 1914. His uncle, and his father, Seth, continued the family business,
and one of Bob’s first jobs when he returned from World War II was to work for
the family business. A graduate of Washington High School in San Francisco,
Bob enrolled in Stanford University, until he was called to active duty with the
United States Army in 1943. He served in the Pacific Theater, becoming a
sergeant before his honorable discharge in 1945.
He became an Entered Apprentice Mason on June 17, 1948, in Fidelity
Lodge No. 120. Soon thereafter he became a Fellow Craft Mason – on
September 9 of that same year – and on September 23, 1948, he was Raised to
the Sublime Degree of Master Mason. Since Masons in those days had to return
the full form of the proficiency between degrees, Bob would be known as a
“bright” Mason – a Mason who quickly learns the work, and makes it a part of
his life. That certainly described Robert Alan Klinger during his entire life.
Quick to learn, quick to share, and quick to make Freemasonry known to others.
In 1957 he served Fidelity Lodge No. 120 as its Centennial Master.
Both his father and his father-in-law were members of Fidelity, and his father
had served the lodge as Master in 1940. During the last year before his
retirement Bob shared many of his experiences as a leader of San Francisco
Freemasonry with his friends, and the stories he told of the Craft during those
halcyon days of the 1950’s and early 1960’s were an inspiration to later
generations of leaders.
Bob later served as an Inspector, and then, in 1969, he came to work for
Grand Lodge, first as the Administrator for Member Services, and then as
Assistant Grand Secretary. Those eleven years were years of growth and
understanding of our Grand Lodge – an understanding which served him well
when he was elected as Grand Secretary in 1980. He went on to serve for
eleven more years as Grand Secretary, earning the love and respect of Masons

28

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

throughout the jurisdiction. His was a very hard act to follow when he finally
retired at the close of the Annual Communication in 1991. For years after his
retirement, Masons throughout California spoke fondly of Bob’s friendship, and
of his loyalty to Grand Lodge.
Although Freemasonry was a very important part of Bob’s life, his
family was even more important. On January 26, 1947, he married Marilyn
Gilbert. They have two children, Steven A. Klinger and Kathy F. Krieg. Bob
was the proud grandfather of four grandchildren, Kendra, Travis, Evan and Josh.
And there is one great-grandson – Nolan.
Bob was deeply loved not only by his family, but by his Masonic
brethren as well. He loved people – and people loved him. He had a wonderful
sense of humor, and a ready joke was always close at hand. At Bob’s funeral,
his son, Steve, shared a special thought about his father – and with this thought
we will close this tribute to the life of Robert Alan Klinger.
We Do Not Lose the Ones We Love
They Only Go Before
Where there is Everlasting Life
Where Sorrow is No More
And There the Soul Will Always Live
And Peace is Everywhere
We Do Not Lose the Ones We Love
God Takes Them in His Care.

Most Worshipful R. Stephen Doan, Past Grand Master, delivered a
eulogy for the late Past Grand Master, Most Worshipful Myron E. Smith.

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

29

EULOGY
MYRON ENGLISH SMITH
Myron English Smith was born in Dixon, Illinois, on December 2,
1906, the son of Allen N. Smith and Mamie English. Myron’s father was what
we would call today a real estate broker and developer. Myron attended the
Dixon public schools. In the early 1920’s, while in high school, Myron worked
for a local grocer and delivered to customers’ homes their purchases. One
family he particularly remembered was John and Nellie Reagan, particularly
Mrs. Reagan whom Myron remembered as being very kind to him when he
brought the groceries to her kitchen table. Of course, Nellie Reagan’s youngest
son Ronnie became the fortieth President of the United States.
After graduating from North Dixon High School in 1925, Myron
moved to Los Angeles where he attended UCLA which then was located on
Vermont at the present site of Los Angeles City College. While at UCLA,
Myron was an active member of the debating team. His parents and younger
brother Allen also moved to Los Angeles, living on Highland Avenue. The
family attended Wilshire Methodist Church.
After graduating from UCLA in 1929, the year that the campus moved
to Westwood, Myron first attended Law School at Stanford University and then
transferred to USC School of Law where he graduated with an LL.B. in 1932.
He was admitted a member of the State Bar on June 10, 1932 and remained a
member for almost seventy-five years! After two years in private practice,
Myron joined the law firm which practiced within the Union Oil Company.
Today, we would call such an arrangement corporate general counsel. Many
members of the legal department at Union Oil were Masons, including L. C.
Kelley who was then a District Inspector and would later become Grand Master.
Myron spent the remainder of his full time legal career at Union Oil, although
later he took a part time corporate counsel position with the Forest Lawn
entities.
Myron married Katherine Joplin from Bloomington, Illinois in 1934.
Son Myron Junior was born in 1936, son Greg in 1945 and daughter Sandy in
1949. The family lived in Sierra Madre for many years.
While at UCLA, Myron joined the fraternity in University Lodge No.
394, now Torrance University Lodge No. 394 in Torrance, on May 14, 1928,
less than six months after he had turned twenty-one. He was Master of his lodge
in 1941. He then became a member of the board of what we now know as the
Los Angeles Masonic Service Bureau. In 1947, he joined the board of Grand
Lodge’s Masonic Clubhouse at UCLA. From that board, he moved to the board
of the Masonic Homes of California on which he served for nine years, the last
four as Vice President. He was remembered as a most effective Grand Orator in
1961. The following year, he was elected Junior Grand Warden, progressing to
become our 100th Grand Master of Masons in October of 1965. Myron

30

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

remained active on Grand Lodge committees past his ninetieth birthday, his last
year of service being on the Grand Lodge Retirement Pension Plan committee.
He received his Diamond Jubilee Seventy-five Year Award in 2003 at his lodge,
giving a most interesting extemporaneous address after the presentation.
Myron joined the Los Angeles Scottish Rite in 1944 and was
unexcelled as a ritualist. He was a key figure in the erection of the Los Angeles
Scottish Rite Cathedral on Wilshire Boulevard. He was coroneted a 33° Mason
in January of 1954.
Myron was very active in the community. He served many charities
associated with the Methodist Church: board member of the Sierra Madre
Methodist Church, President of the Pasadena Methodist District Union, board
member of Pacific Homes, board member of Methodist Hospital in Arcadia and
board member of Goodwill Industries. He served more than forty-five years on
the board of the Midnight Mission, several as President. Myron’s brother Allen,
a Master Mason himself, was also noted for public service, serving as a Special
Agent for the FBI after he graduated from law school, and then serving in the
California State Assembly for eight years and then the United States House of
Representatives from Glendale for sixteen years.
After the passing of Myron’s wife Kay in 1972, he married and spent
the rest of his life with Mary Frances Sharon whom he had met when she and
her late husband owned a local Marie Callender’s restaurant.
We counted Myron for many years among the senior Past Grand
Masters of this jurisdiction, and Myron demonstrated the characteristics we
would want from that unique leadership role. Myron’s advice was always well
reasoned and articulate, but his other contributions were more significant. He
was a mentor: identifying, recruiting and encouraging younger men for Grand
Lodge leadership. He was a visionary: he was not content with what we have
done since time immemorial, but sought how we could make our traditions
responsive to contemporary challenges. Above all, Myron was always an
exemplar of our Masonic teaching of who best can work and best agree. Myron
often said, “Speak of a brother to praise him.” The worst that Myron ever said
about any Mason was, “Dear man.”
Myron celebrated his one hundredth birthday in good health and spirits
with his family on December 2, 2006. After a brief decline, he passed on July
22, 2007 at his home in Phoenix, Arizona. A giant among California Masons,
Myron’s greatest legacy will be the inspiration to excel which he left those who
knew him.

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

MYRON E. SMITH
PAST GRAND MASTER
December 2, 1906 – July 22, 2007

Memorial Service Prayer
Heavenly Father, we ask you to remember the lives of our Brothers
who have come to be with you this past year. We thank you for the gift of
memory as we think of the days that our Brothers were with us and we enjoyed
the blessings of their companionship and affection. The departed whom we now
remember have entered into the peace of life eternal. They still live on earth in
the acts of goodness they performed and in the hearts of those who cherish their
memory. May the beauty of their life abide among us as a loving benediction.
May your Light shine on them and us forever. Amen.
G.

Grand Master's Project Recognition
The Grand Master’s project for this last year addresses important issues
of the past, present and future. The funds raised will create the Heritage Fund, a
permanently restricted fund dedicated to organizing, preserving and exhibiting
artifacts relative to Masonic heritage. To date this project has raised $150,000.
In addition, the Grand Master’s wife, Betty, took on a project of her
own – raising funds for Operation Homefront. These funds will assist enlisted
men and women in the Armed Forces who receive limited pay. Over $30,000
has been donated to this special project this year.

The Bush Foundation, Most Worshipful Melville H. Nahin, PGM,
representing the foundation.
Fresno Lodge No. 247, Worshpful David A. Coleman, Master,
representing the Lodge.
Grand Master Stein then gave his closing remarks and expressed his
honor and privilege at having been given the opportunity to serve the Craft as
Grand Master. He expressed his thanks to his wife, Betty, for her support of him
and California Masons.
The 2007 Grand Lodge Officers then retired from the auditorium while
the Assistant Grand Organist played “Thanks for the Memories” followed by a
brief interlude prior to the Installation of the 2007/2008 Grand Lodge Officers.

39

GRAND MASTER’S MESSAGE
Well, good afternoon Brethren. At many Masonic events this year, and
in the California Freemason, I began with the words form a 1940’s radio
commentator, Gabriel Heater who opened his news program with the words
“There’s good news tonight!” We can summarize this year with the same words
and the concept that Masonry is on the move in California.
We have performed more degrees in the last year than in any year in
the last eight years. The average age of applicants for Masonry is 39.5 years.
Masonry continued on the move this year when the number o
application exceeded the anticipated number by over 30%.
Immediately on the day following my installation, a scheduled meeting
was held with six key committee chairmen requesting them to present their
prepared plans, goals and methods for achieving those objectives. After
discussion, each agreed to a date to report back. Each has done so, and good
progress has been made.
This year I have emphasized that mutual communication is essential in
assuring that the Brethren, individual lodges, Inspectors and Assistant Grand
Lecturers are conveying their thoughts to the Grand Lodge and vice versa.
To aid in this process of understanding, I met on the second day
following installation with the Grand Lecturer, Assistant Grand Lectures, the
progressive Grand Lodge line, Grand Secretary, Assistant Grand Secretary and
Grand Treasurer to openly discuss issues and concerns. I instituted quarterly of
this group to assure all were being heard. This has resulted in a renewed sense
of cooperation. I believe that continued mutual communication, understanding
and harmony will prevail if this procedure is followed in the future.
Another policy begun this year has been the personal interview by an
elected progressive Grand Lodge line officer of all recommendees for new
Inspectors. This assures that we are all working for the benefit of Freemasonry
in California.
In order to track lodges which may need help or assistance in
membership, lodge planning, finances or other issues, the Lodge Support
Committee was reconstituted to include such assistance when requested to do so.
This year the membership of your Masonic Properties Committee was
substantially reinforced, and each member underwent a training course focusing
on real property issues and Hall Associations.
A new Grand Lodge Long Term Planning Committee was formed with
well known and experienced members of the Craft. These Brethren were tasked
to take on all aspects of our Fraternity and to “think outside the box”. Their

40

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

report this August was substantial and requires further in depth work. Their
recommendations will lead to modifications in future planning.
As a result of my previous request, a special committee to discuss and
revise curriculum for the 1  day mandatory Inspector’s seminars was created.
As part of the Inspector’s seminars this Masonic year, I included in the
curriculum the “Elevator Speech”. Masons have long been confronted with the
inability, in a 20 second reply, to answer the question “What is masonry?” when
asked in an elevator or passing comment. There is no time to be philosophical
or explain the ritual or degrees. The response could simply be, “Freemasonry is
the oldest fraternal organization in the world making good men better for his
family, his country and all mankind. The “Golden Rule” is our basic tenet”.
Each brother should be ready to give a knowledgeable short response to “What
is Freemasonry?”
A new online quarterly reporting system for Inspectors has been put
into place on the website for easy and more effective reporting. No more hand
written or individually typed reports.
At the Conference of Grand Masters in Oregon, we previewed our
online E-Learning Lodge Certification Program on lodge finance, which is now
in place on our website. This internet experience permits any Brother, at any
time, to access materials from his home or office over any period of time; and is
so sophisticated that when you complete the course and pass the test you receive
a certificate of completion by email which is automatically registered at Grand
Lodge.
Because of the importance of the Masonic veterans returning from Iraq
and Afghanistan, as well as our aging military Brethren, I upgraded those who
regularly visited our Veteran’s Administration Hospitals to a full committee
status with emphasis on Brethren to cover all such hospitals and to submit
quarterly reports of activities to the Grand Lodge. The Grand Master will be
continuing to work with this committee next year.
Each state leader of our four youth orders was contacted by an elected
progressive Grand Lodge Officer to ascertain how grand Lodge, and the lodges
of California, can be of help and assistance. We have received, at our request;
three of the four youth orders five-year plans. We have also received the
requested copies of their adult leadership training programs as they relate to
adults dealing with young people.
The new California Masonic Code was not only printed and distributed;
it has been placed on our administrative website for easy referencing and
indexing.
In the area of Masonic education we are completing a DVD to be given
to the First Degree recipient to take home to his family and friends explaining

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

41

the significance of Masonry and the role it can play in their lives. A non-final
version will be presented to you on Saturday before lunch.
The Grand Master’s special project this year has been the preservation,
restoration and exhibition of our Masonic historic artifacts. A small part of our
collection is on display here this weekend. Through your generosity and those
we will honor at the public ceremonies on Sunday, we have received over
$150,000.
My wife, Betty, expresses her appreciation to those who provided funds
for her special project called “Operation Home Front”. The funds will assist
enlisted men and women in our Armed Forces who receive limited pay. By the
end of the month, particularly in emergencies, their pay doesn’t quite cover their
expenses. “Operation Home Front” provides these needed funds and material
items without any administrative costs. Betty will present over $30,000 to this
organization on behalf of the Masons of California.
Your elected Grand Lodge Officers are wearing their new solid gold
jewels and new aprons and collars. A new Grand Lodge banner was brought in
for the first time this afternoon. We extend our special appreciation to Right
Worshipful Larry Adamson, Sylvia McBride, wife of our Assistant Grand
Lecturer A. Glenn McBride and Robin Rose, wife of our Assistant Grand
Lecturer Jack Rose, in making this a reality.
Your Grand Master, accompanied by the Grand Secretary, had the
privilege to represent the Grand Lodge of California at the World Conference of
Grand Masters held in Paris, France last November. As this was purely a
Masonic matter, our wives did not accompany us. Over 95 Grand Masters from
around the world attended (with only five from the United States). It was my
honor on your behalf to present simultaneously translated in four languages, the
only written paper from the United States.
At the joint Supreme Council session of the Scottish Rite in
Washington D.C., a close relationship between the Scottish Rite and 46 Grand
Lodges present was developed. The astronaut, Brother John Glenn, at age 86,
proved to be an inspiring speaker.
However, the highlight of my travels this year has been to be received
with honor and dignity by many Brethren attending Masonic functions
throughout this state. You only begin to understand the size of California when
you travel to many of the lodges. At each event I met dedicated and devoted
masons practicing our Masonic principles and a large number of young men
entering our fraternity with enthusiasm and a desire to master the philosophy of
our ritual and how it can affect their daily lives. This was the most encouraging
and enjoyable part of my term as Grand Master. I thank you for permitting me
to enjoy this once in a lifetime opportunity to be of service to our Craft.

42

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

As I began this year with Gabriel Heater’s “There’s good news
tonight”, I now conclude my term with the closing words of news broadcaster,
Walter Cronkite, “and that’s the way it is” on this Saturday, September 29, two
thousand and seven.
Respectfully submitted,
Melvyn B. Stein, Grand Master

REPORT OF THE DEPUTY GRAND MASTER
2007/2008 Grand Lodge Budget
Richard W. Hopper, Deputy Grand Master

As Deputy Grand Master, it is my duty pursuant to California Masonic
Code Section 7040, to prepare and submit at the Annual Communication of
Grand Lodge a budget based upon the approved per capita set the previous year
by vote of the Grand Lodge.
The attached proposed Grand Lodge Budget for the period November
1, 2007 through October 31, 2008 has been carefully prepared and reviewed and
is recommended by your Grand Treasurer, Grand Secretary, Assistant Grand
Secretary, qualified Finance Department staff, and me as Deputy Grand Master.
It has been unanimously approved by your Brethren who serve on the Grand
Lodge Finance Committee and achieved after a study of historical operating data
and reasonable projections of income and expense for the next year. It is based
on information considered to be quite accurate.
This Grand Lodge Budget is funded by a per capita of $29.00 and is
based on a membership of 63,720 Master Masons together with income from
fees, interest and a Grant from the California Masonic Foundation of $138,000
to fund our increasing efforts to provide Masonic Education and Leadership
Training. Any such grant funds not used will be returned to the Foundation.
For the 2007-2008 Masonic year we will continue to develop our
Lodge Management Certification Programs by adding online training
components and, thus, permitting completion at home at the convenience of the
Brethren. New programs will continue to be developed in an effort to cause
candidates who have not been progressing in their degree work to do so. There
will be continuing efforts in the area of Masonic Formation to meet the needs
and desires of our Craft Brethren and those who may be seeking information
about our Fraternity. Masonry in California is reaching out through the use of
today’s communication media.
We are aware that many of our lodges are struggling in their operations
and, in some instances, are having a hard time even surviving in their
communities. We are dedicating funds and many of our talented Brethren are
volunteering to assist such Lodges in working through their difficulties. These
may be in such areas as membership development, membership retention,
leadership development, community outreach, financial management, and Hall
Association operations.
Recognizing the importance of Inspectors, we are asking all of them to
attend the annual Inspectors Administrative Seminar where they will receive
information that will assist them in their duties and bring them up to date with
regard to information being taught at our annual Wardens Leadership Retreats.

60

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

These seminars will include information requested by the Inspectors based on
their relationship with you, the leaders of your lodges.
The majority of our per capita income is being spent on services to
members, Lodges, and Inspectors. Great efforts have been made in the areas of
expense reduction, proper allocation of costs that are shared with the other
entities of our overall Grand Lodge operation, and salaries that are based on
market studies of salaries paid in the professional market place. We continue to
cash fund our depreciation in order to create reserves for replacements.
The salaries of the Grand Secretary and Assistant Grand Secretary are
set forth under General and Administrative expense. The figure in the right
hand column is the net figure supported by the per capita. Since these two
Brothers are responsible for the administration of all Masonic corporate entities
in California, the cost is off-set in large part by payment from those other
Masonic entities. It should be noted that there are a total of eight full time
equivalent persons working directly for the Grand Lodge to support our 342
Lodges with total membership of just under 64,000 Master Masons plus several
thousand Entered Apprentices and Fellowcrafts.
Thank you for your support last year in approving the Per Capita
Budget that I prepared and presented to you. That support has allowed me to
develop this current budget document. I herewith present to you a balanced
budget which will sustain our efforts to make good men into outstanding family,
Lodge, and community leaders so we may continue to say that, in California,
“Freemasonry is Standing Strong”.

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

INSTALLATION ADDRESS
RICHARD WAKEFIELD HOPPER
Grand Master of Masons in California, 2007-2008
While it would appear that much honor has come to me personally, I do
realize that this Fraternity has raised many men who had the ability to be a
Grand Master and I am humbled that God has made the current selection.
God made another selection some 43 years ago. He brought Judi and
me together. I am humbled by that move, also. What I did to deserve that gift is
still beyond me. She will be a great First Lady. I can’t do this job without her; I
don’t want to do this job without her.
I accept the tasks of being Grand Master, just as I love the First Lady,
with a huge amount of passion.
Recently, it seems to have become a tradition for the Grand Lodge
officers to use their middle initials instead of their whole name. I am breaking
that tradition this year and asking that at formal introductions my whole middle
name of Wakefield be used.
I am doing this to honor a very special person in my life, my
grandfather, Chester Kenneth Wakefield, 33rd Degree Inspector General
Honorary. Best known as “Pop”, he was well known for his generosity, his
involvement in the lives of troubled youth, his commitment to Masonry and his
sense of humor. When asked, at the age of 85, how he was feeling, his response
was, “I’m doing great. I just bought a mattress with a ten year guarantee.” His
role in my life cannot be overestimated. If someone carried your name, would
they be proud?
I wish to say “thank you” to several people at this time. I am so
grateful that Judi is walking along side of me on this Grand Master’s trail.
And right behind us are our kids and our grandkids. Our daughter,
Jana, has been a great help and inspiration to us. Our son, Keith, took over
ownership and operation of our business in order that I might retire and work
full time for this Fraternity. Along side of Keith is our daughter-in-law, Kristin,
who supports Keith in the business and is a Registered Nurse in her own right.
Keith and Kristin are Mom and Dad to our two wonderful
grandchildren, Kathryn and Kristopher. Kathryn and Kris have been so
understanding of the fact that Grandma and Grandpa have not been at some of
their academic, music, and athletic events.
Las Palmas Ponderosa Lodge No. 366 in Fresno. I was raised a Master
Mason in this lodge where my two grandfathers were both members, my Dad
was Master in 1950, and my brother, Larry, was Master in 1984. Also from this
lodge, I must thank Most Worshipful Sam Pavlovich, PGM, for being my coach

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

69

and for appointing me to the lodge officers’ line when he was Worshipful
Master.
Visalia Mineral King Lodge No. 128 in Visalia. I was granted a plural
membership in this lodge in the 1990s when then Worshipful Master Ken
Sheffield asked me to be his Chaplain. I later was given the opportunity to serve
the lodge in 1998 as its Master. Wor. Ken and Wor. Bill Michalk, in particular,
were instrumental in encouraging me to progress in Masonry during those years.
I am thankful for being given the opportunity to serve Grand Lodge as
the Jr. Grand Deacon under MW C. Ray Whitaker. Then MW Bill Holsinger
appointed me to the Board of Trustees of the Masonic Homes of California and
granted me the chance to work with Board President MW Allen Gresham and
other well educated, experienced, and professional brethren of our Craft.
I have had many strong mentors over the past several years, particularly
four Grand Masters that I served under: MWs Howard Kirkpatrick, David
Doan, Fred Sorsabal, and Mel Stein. Thank you my Brethren for your patience
and your teaching.
Finally, I have had the support over the last three years from three of
our brothers who have shared their thoughts, ideas, and leadership styles with
me. They are Larry Adamson, Ken Nagel, and Bill Bray, who will walk along
this Grand Master trail in the years to follow. They are the Allstate Brothers
because with their leadership in the future, you are in good hands.
I have chosen the Giant Sequoia Redwood tree as the Emblem for this
grand year and the Theme “Freemasonry Standing Strong”. I selected them not
only because my hometown of Visalia is the gateway to Sequoia National Park
where these majestic giants live and grow, but because they have many parallels
to Masonry.
As our brotherhood is the oldest and largest fraternity in the world, this
stately evergreen is one of the oldest living trees on earth and the General
Sherman Tree in Sequoia National Park is recognized as the largest of its
species. One would expect that trees of this size and volume would have
massive root systems that reach deeply into California’s rich soil. Instead, their
root systems are relatively shallow. Alone, they could not withstand the ravages
of time, weather, or erosion. They are capable of standing only when they grow
in groves and allow their roots to intertwine with others. Together they gain
strength and can support each other.
I’m sure you can see the lesson for Masonry here today. We must
stand together against the ravages of misinformation, misunderstanding and lack
of commitment. We must stand together as a band of men willing to better
ourselves. And, we must never forget our roots.
When our roots reach out and embrace each other, then, and only then,
will Freemasonry truly be Standing Strong.

70

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

The Strategic Plan of your Grand Lodge states that our Mission is to
understand and convey the wisdom of Masonic teachings to constituent lodges
in a way that engages and inspires members to conduct their lives according to
Masonic tenets and values.
We have programs in place to support our Mission’s initiatives and we
will continue to develop and expand them in the year ahead, but I want to direct
our attention to just one of these programs this morning.
It is our Children’s Program on our Masonic Homes campus in Covina.
We have for the past few years been operating a program known as the Circle
of Courage. It focuses on two areas. The children live on our campus and,
during their residency, they are taught lessons in right living. At the same time,
their parents and/or guardians are required to attend parenting classes on a
regular basis on the campus. We take this approach because it takes both
children and parents to make a functional family in today’s society.
In years past, our children have resided in our Homes for periods of
time exceeding five years and, in many instances, for much longer times. With
the Circle of Courage program, the average residency time is two and a half
years. It is a program that is working! It is reunifying families! But, it is also a
program that could be easily expanded.
The 2008 Grand Family Project will focus on expanding this program.
With assistance from the California Masonic Foundation and caring Masons
such as each of you, we will be able to expand the program to reach more
children and their families, to reunify them, and to send them back into their
communities as functioning and contributing family units.
With an Emblem of Giant Sequoia Trees, what else could we name this
project other than Project T-R-E-E (Toward Reunification, Education, and
Enrichment). I ask each of you to take this Grand Family Project back to your
lodges and to encourage your Brethren at home to join in support of it.
Imagine this situation: a father is talking to his teenage son and the
conversation goes something like this. “Young man, I don’t know how many
times I’ve told you to clean up this room and yet it is messy day after day. If
you don’t get this room cleaned up, you are not going anywhere this weekend”.
And the young man looks at his Dad and just says “Whatever”!
“Whatever” is, at times, a term of sarcasm and we find it creeping into
our voices and thinking all too often in our lodges and in our personal lives. If
things do not go the exact way that we want them to, we adopt a “whatever”
attitude rather than make an attempt to understand another’s viewpoint.
The “whatevers” in our lodges should never sway us from our basic
tenets of Brotherly Love, Relief, and Truth. We just need to deal with them with
an attitude of fraternal love and respect. We need to allow them to teach us and
draw us closer to one another.

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

71

Whatever happens, whoever you are in relationship with, conduct
yourselves in a manner worthy of our Masonic teachings.
If we do, we cannot be defeated; we can do amazing things. We will be
seen as a Fraternity that lives out what it believes; as a group of men who put
faith in God, family, country, neighbors, and fraternal brethren before
themselves.
May I suggest that in all we think, and say, and do, we should
remember this phrase, “Whatever…….Walk Worthy”.
Finally, my Brethren and friends, I share the following story with you.
Wishing to encourage her young son’s progress on the piano, a mother
took her boy to a Paderewski concert. After they were seated, the mother
spotted a friend in the audience and walked down the aisle to greet her.
Seizing the opportunity to explore the wonders of the concert hall, the
little boy rose and eventually explored his way through a door marked “NO
ADMITTANCE”. When the house lights dimmed and the concert was about to
begin, the mother returned to her seat and discovered that the child was missing.
Suddenly, the curtains parted and spotlights focused on the impressive
Steinway on stage. In horror, the mother saw her little boy sitting at the
keyboard, innocently picking out “Twinkle, Twinkle Little Star”.
At that moment, the great piano master made his entrance, quickly
moved to the piano, and whispered in the boy’s ear, “Don’t quit. Keep playing”.
Then leaning over, Paderewski reached down with his left hand and
began filling in a bass part. Soon his right arm reached around to the other side
of the child and he added a running obbligato. Together, the old master and the
young novice transformed a frightening situation into a wonderfully creative
experience. The audience was mesmerized.
That’s the way it is in life. What we can accomplish on our own is
hardly noteworthy. We try our best, but the results aren’t exactly graceful
flowing music. But when we trust in the hands of a Greater Power, our life’s
work truly can be beautiful.
Next time you set out to accomplish great feats, listen carefully. You
can hear the voice of God, the Master, whispering in your ear, “Don’t quit.
Keep playing”.

REPORT OF THE SENIOR GRAND WARDEN
Per Capita Recommendation for 2008-2009
Larry L. Adamson, Senior Grand Warden

Each year your Senior Grand Warden is responsible for proposing the
future per capita assessment adequate to fund the obligations of our Grand
Lodge during his tenure as your Grand Master. During the past decade, this
process has been expanded greatly and substantial time and expertise have been
added. This year is no exception.
The present system of budget projections must include forecasted
future inflationary factors, resources to support strategic initiatives, and most
importantly, 2008 membership projections. This system begins a full four years
before an actual budget is finalized and is presented to the Grand Lodge at our
Annual Communication. This comprehensive and exhaustive budgetary process
means that the budget upon which the per capita is based is as accurate in
forecasting our needs as is possible.
Accordingly, it is my intent to present to the Grand Lodge at our
Annual Communication a forecasted revenue and expense budget of $2,446,200
and request a supporting per capita of $31.00 per Master Mason. The Grand
Lodge Finance Committee, Grand Treasurer, Grand Secretary, Assistant Grand
Secretary and Deputy Grand Master have met either individually or collectively
with me to review the proposed budget for 2008/2009 and all have agreed with
me that this is a fair representation of our financial needs to support our future
obligations.
There are several factors that have a substantial effect on the budget
and the need for our revenue assessment known as “per capita.” The most
significant factor in the past four decades has been membership levels. As we
are all aware, a declining membership places disproportional pressure on the
amount of the per capita assessment. Although the past several years are very
encouraging, as it relates to membership activity in our lodges, the 2008/2009
analysis projects a loss in membership of 2,770 members. While this is the
lowest loss of members in decades, the membership decline will result in lost
revenue of just over $80,000 or $1.32 on a per capita basis. Inflation, which is a
constant concern has been considered in this request and is projected at just
2.75%. Taken together, these two factors alone would dictate to us an increase
in per capita of more than the $31.00 that I am proposing. The attached
“Revenue Assumptions” exhibit will give you a fuller explanation of how I have
forecasted all our revenue categories for the 2008/2009 year. It is important to
note that this $2.00 increase in per capita revenue results in only a 2% increase
in the total per capita collected from the previous year and less than the overall
inflationary impact assumed in my budget.

76

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

My budget request also includes your support of a number of
continuing and four new strategic initiatives taken directly from our Grand
Lodge Strategic Plan, which I believe are essential to the immediate support of
our lodges and the future well-being of our craft. Please take the time to review
the details of these as outlined in the attached “Strategic Initiatives” exhibit.
I believe that California Freemasonry is essential to the well-being of
our society today and that we are poised to experience a resurgence of interest in
our craft. I believe it is essential that our Grand Lodge remains focused on
supporting this future need. This budget request is focused squarely on that
target and your support will help to ensure that Masonic virtues remain alive and
that we all stay focused on building for a better tomorrow.

REPORT OF THE GRAND TREASURER
To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F.& A.M. of California:
The California Masonic Code, Section 404.230 (F), directs the Grand
Treasurer to “prepare and deliver, at each Annual Communication, a detailed
report of the financial condition of Grand Lodge, prepared in accordance with
generally accepted accounting principles on the accrual method of accounting,
using a fiscal year ending October 31st”. That section further directs that such
report shall have been reviewed by the Committee on Finance and shall have
been audited by outside auditors.
To strictly comply with that direction, I would now be giving you
information as of October 31, 2006, some eleven months old. I do not believe
such stale information would be useful to you today and, if it is, you can find it
in the published Proceedings of the 2006 Annual Communication.
The information I am going to give you today is for a nine-month
period from the start of this current fiscal year, November 1, 2006, through July
31, 2007. I will compare that information to like information for the same period
a year earlier. Further, this is already posted on the Administrative website under
Forms/Annual Communication. It will continue to be available to you there until
the official proceedings of this Communication are published. Those
Proceedings will contain the customary audited financials for the entire fiscal
year.
It should be noted that this partial-year information has been prepared
internally by our fine financial staff and has not been audited.
As of July 31, 2007, your Grand Lodge had assets of $5,435,308 and
liabilities of $644,706, for a Net Worth of $4,790,602. Those assets were some
31% higher than at the same date a year earlier, largely due to increases in
investment accounts, receivables, and equipment. Keep in mind that most of the
assets you might attach to the California Grand Lodge are not owned by the
Grand Lodge itself and do not appear in these numbers. I am referring here to
major assets such as this facility, which is owned by the California Masonic
Memorial Temple Corp. and our homes in Union City and Covina, which are
owned by the Masonic Homes of California, both separate corporations.
Due to the diligent oversight by the Investment Committee, the
investment accounts of our Masonic entities had an annual gain of 16.4% for the
12 months ending July 31, 2007 and a three-year annualized return of 12.9%, as
of the same date.
Your Grand Lodge continues to operate in the black. For the ninemonths ending July 31, 2007, the total income was $1,727,520, while total
expenses were $1,286,334. That income is some 1.27% higher than the

84

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

comparative period last year. Expenses for the nine-month period ending July
31, 2007 were also higher than the same period last year, a difference that is
largely due to the timing of various expenses. While the Net Excess as of July
31, 2007 is some $441,000, it should be noted that approximately 20% of the
total year’s expenses occur in the last quarter of the year. The projection is that
the Grand Lodge will end the fiscal year on October 31, with a slight surplus.
Being new to this position this past year, I have been most impressed
with the concern and diligence which your elected Executive Committee and the
financial staff bring to the monitoring of the actual income and expenses versus
the budget. The numbers are compiled and studied by all parties in great detail
monthly, as the year progresses. You should know that any surplus showing at
the end of the fiscal year is not carried over into the next year, but rather it is
permanently transferred to the Endowment Fund. The result is that each Grand
Master starts his year with a zero balance, with only the budget for that year to
work with.
The nine-month expenses were 28% for Fraternal matters (such as this
Annual Communication, travel expenses of the Elected and Appointed Grand
Lodge Officers and Ritual Committee and the insurance for the constituent
lodges), 18% for Appropriations and Programs (such as various lodge support
Committees, the Freemason magazine, and Committees such as Policy,
Jurisprudence, Investment and Trial Review), and 49% to General and
Administrative items (such as salaries and benefits, accounting and auditing,
investment management fees, legal fees and general office operations). That
breakdown compares closely to the same period last year. General and
Administrative Expenses are some higher than a year ago, largely due to the use
of temporary employees to fill vacancies in the permanent staff.
A separate internal Audit Committee has been appointed this year
whose members do not serve on the Board or Finance Committee of any of our
entities. This arrangement is in conformance with the CMC and accepted
accounting and financial standards. Its function is to select the independent
auditors for our various entities and then to review the resulting audit reports, as
they are presented by those auditors.
Due to my involvement in all of the business operations of the various
Masonic entities in California, I sit in numerous Board and Committee meetings
and have a front-row seat from which to witness the tremendous amount of time,
effort, concern and dedication applied to the vast amount of good work being
carried on by the Masons of California. My wish would be that each of you
could have just a small glimpse of that good work. It would make you
immensely proud to be a California Mason. My hat is off to each of those
dedicated Masons who serve on those Boards and Committees.

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

85

I must also pay tribute to the efficient and dedicated staff in our Grand
Lodge office and at the Union City and Covina campuses. Under the leadership
of Grand Secretary Very Worshipful John Cooper and Assistant Grand Secretary
Worshipful Allan Casalou, their ability, effort, long hours and devotion to their
work is above and beyond and, is much appreciated by this Grand Treasurer.
Respectfully submitted,
Glenn D. Woody, Grand Treasurer

REPORT OF THE GRAND SECRETARY
My brethren, this is my sixteenth message to you as your Grand
Secretary. Before delivering my report, I would like to express my appreciation
to our Grand Master, Most Worshipful Melvyn B. Stein, for his support this past
year, together with that of all of his Grand Lodge Officers and committeemen.
An important purpose of the Grand Lodge Office is to provide support to the
Grand Master and his officers, and to the various committees of Grand Lodge.
That has been an easy task because of our Grand Master’s leadership, and a
pleasant one on all occasions.
The Grand Secretary also serves as Secretary to all the several boards
of Grand Lodge: The Masonic Homes, the California Masonic Memorial
Temple, the Nob Hill Masonic Center, and the California Masonic Foundation.
To this have been recently added additional responsibilities as Secretary to the
two Acacia Creek boards, and to the Board of Directors of the Institute for
Masonic Studies. Each of these boards has significant responsibilities for major
programs of Grand Lodge, and my thanks also goes to the presidents of these
boards for their support this past year for your Grand Secretary, and for all the
staff of the Grand Lodge Office. However, above all, I want to extend my
personal appreciation for the leadership of our Assistant Grand Secretary,
Worshipful Bro. Allan L. Casalou, whose leadership in many areas of endeavor
on your behalf has resulted in significant achievements. He managed the
business operations of our California Masonic Memorial Temple, in an
exemplary manner, and accomplished this while serving as Executive Director
of Grand Lodge. In addition, he led our leadership training programs, including
the Wardens’ Retreats, and also found time to assist our Senior Grand Warden in
creating the first-ever on-line management training course in lodge finance. I
would like you in extending to Worshipful Bro. Allan a warm round of applause
for all his accomplishments!
I was honored this year by being selected by other Grand Secretaries
across the United States and Canada as the President of the Conference of Grand
Secretaries. Next February I will preside over that conference in Louisville,
Kentucky. The only other California Grand Secretary so honored was Most
Worshipful Edward G. Siems, our Grand Secretary from 1960 to 1977. This
honor, my brethren, is yours as well. California is highly esteemed amongst the
jurisdictions of North America, and I am privileged to represent you in this high
office this year.
Grand Master Stein asked me to lead the Masonic Formation Task
Force this year, an appointment I shared with our Grand Lecturer. Masonic
Formation, simply stated, is making Freemasonry come alive for our members –
and especially for our new Entered Apprentices, Fellow Crafts, and Master
Masons, who will be the future of the Craft in the generation to come. Its focus,

90

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

indeed its primary purpose, is to enable Masons to take out of the lodge room
the great teachings inculcated in it. Whether called “Applied Masonry”, or
“Masonry for Today,” it is Masonry that makes a difference for each Mason,
each Lodge, and for the community in which that Mason and that Lodge
practices Masonry. Special thanks this year goes to Bro. Ron Martin, who
helped move Masonic Formation forward in an effective manner.
The Proceedings will contain other reports from the Grand Lodge
Office this year, including membership statistics. Suffice it to say that we
continue to initiate more Entered Apprentice Masons every year than the year
before. The future of Freemasonry in California is bright, and you and I are
privileged to live in a time in which Freemasonry will change the nature and
future of our society.
It is also a responsibility of the Grand Secretary to advise the Grand
Master on matters pertaining to other Grand Lodges, and in particular, Grand
Lodges seeking recognition from the Grand Lodge of California. In 2003 this
Grand Lodge offered to extend recognition to Prince Hall Grand Lodges in those
“mainstream” jurisdictions which also recognized the Prince Hall Grand Lodge
in their jurisdiction, thereby sharing territory. We have now recognized twentyseven Prince Hall Grand Lodges in the United States – a significant number,
because some Prince Hall Grand Lodges cover more than one state. This list
will undoubtedly grow as more and more “mainstream” Grand Lodges recognize
the regularity and legitimacy of Prince Hall Freemasonry.
In addition, we have received requests for recognition from several
other Grand Lodges, and – after having taking into account the recommendation
of the Commission on Information for Recognition Conference of Grand
Masters of Masons in North America in 2007, I move that the following Grand
Lodge be accorded full and complete recognition by our Grand Lodge:
The Grand Lodge of Ukraine
Finally, one of my responsibilities as Grand Secretary is to supervise
the requirements of our code for the formation of new lodges, and the issuance
or charters to them. It is with great pleasure that I inform you that Siminoff
Daylight Lodge, Under Dispensation, has fulfilled all the requirements to
become a regular lodge in this jurisdiction. I therefore move that Siminoff
Daylight Lodge be issued a charter as Siminoff Daylight Lodge No. 850.
Most Worshipful, this concludes my report as your Grand Secretary for
the 158th Annual Communication.

Respectfully submitted,
John L. Cooper III, Grand Secretary

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

Lodge Consolidations
All requirements of the law with respect to consolidation of Lodges
having been complied with, authorization is requested to properly endorse the
charters of these Lodges as follows:
Eel River Lodge No. 147 and Redwood Empire Lodge No. 683,
effective January 1, 2007, to be known as Eel River Empire Lodge No. 147.
Landmark Buckeye Lodge No. 195 and Athens-Silveyville Lodge No.
201, effective January 1, 2007, to be known as Yolo Lodge No. 195.
Modoc Lodge No. 235 and Fort Crook Lodge No. 250, effective June
1, 2007, to be known as Fort Crook Lodge No. 250.
Illinoistown Lodge No. 51 and Clay Lodge No. 101, effective
September 1, 2007, to be known as Illinoistown-Clay Lodge No. 51.
